CABINET SECRETARIAT 

(Development of Personnel and Administrative Reforms) 

New Delhi, the 4th September, 1976 

S.O. 3385. — In exercise of the poweis conferred by the 
proviso to article 309 and clause (5) of article 148 of the 
Constitution, and in consultation with he Comptroller and 
Auditor General so far as persons serving in the Indian Audit 
and Accounts Department are concerned, the President hereby 
makes the following rules further to amend the Central Civil 
Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964, namely ;— 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Cent.a, 

( Conduct) Fourth Amendment Rufes, 1976. 

(2) They shall come into force from the date of their 
publication in the Official Gazette. 

2. In the Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964, 
after rule 21, the following rule shall be inserted, namely:— 

“21-A. Adoption of small family by Government 
servants. — Every Government seivant shall ensure 


(3095) 



THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 25, 1976/ASVINA 3, 1898 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii) I 


that the number of his children does not exceed 
three : 

vided that nothing in this rule shall apply to a 
Government servant who has more than three 
-hidren on the 30th day of September, 1977: 

T'TvnfJfd further that a Government servant referred 
to in the preceding proviso shall ensure that the 
number of his children does not exceed the number 
of children he has on that day," 

[No. 11013 /14/76-Estl(A)] 
R. C. GUPTA, Under Secy.

S.O. 3387. — Tn exercise of the powers conferred by sec- 
tion 3 of the Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 
(25 of 1946) the Central Government hereby specifies the 
following offences as the offences which are to be investigated 
by the Delhi Special Police Establishment, namely : — 

(a) Offences punishable under section 25 of the Anti- 
quities and Art Treasures Act, 1972 (52 of 1972); 

^ and 

(b) attempts, abetments and conspiracies in relation to 

or in connection with one or more of the offences 
metloncd in clause (a) above and any other offence 
committed in the course of the same transaction 
arising out of the same facts.

ELECTION COMMISSION OF INDIA 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 2nd September, 1976 

S,0. 3388 — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that ShTi Siyambar, Village Sarmana, P.O. Sedarn, Tehsil 
Ambikapur, District Surguja, Mpdhyn Pradesh who was a 
coniesting candidale for election to the Madhya Pradesh 
Lcgisla I i 1 e Assembly from 72-Premnagar Constituency held 
in March, 1972 has failed to lodge an account of his election 
expenses as required by Ihe Representation of the People Act, 
1951, and the Rules made thereunder ; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the duo notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure aud 
the Election Commission Is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Siyambar to be disqualified for being chosen as, and for being, 
a member of either House of Parliament or of the Legislative 
Assembly or Legislative Council of a State for a period of 
three years from the date of this order. 

By order, 

1 No. MP-LA/72/72(1 16)] 
P. K. MISRA, Secy.

S.O. 3394. — In exercise of the powers conferred by clause 
(1) of article 258 of the Constitution, the President, with 
the consent of the State Government of Sikkini, hereby en- 
trusts to the Government of the State of Sikkim the func- 
tions of Ihe Central Government under section 5 of (he Pass- 
port (Entry into India) Act, 1920 (34 of 1920), and under 
rules 2 and 4 of the Passport (Entry into India) Rules, 1950, 
subject to the following conditions, namely : — ■ 

(a) that in the exercise of such functions the said State 
Government shall comply with such general or 
special directions as the Central Government may 
from time to time issue; and 

(b) that notwithstanding this entrustment, the Central 
Government may itself exercise any of the said 
functions should it deem fit to do so in any case. 

[No. 11011/175-F. I(iii)] 
R. A. S. MANI, Dy. Secy.

MINISTRY OF PLANNING 
(Department of Statistics) 

New Delhi, the 6 th September, 1976 

S.O. 3395. — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (i) of Section 8 of the Indian Statistical Institute Act, 
1959, the Central Government hereby constitutes a Committee 
consisting of : — 

1. Shri P. C. Mathew. — Chairman. 

2. Dr. K. C. Seal, Adviser, Planning Commission, New 
Delhi. — Member, 

3. A representative of the Indian Statistical Institute, 

Calcutta. — Member. 

4. Additional Secretary, Ministry of Finance, Department 
of Expenditure and Financial Adviser to the Deptt. of 
Statistics. — Member. 

5. Director, Central Statistical Orgn, and cx-cffido Addi- 

tional Secretary, Department of Statistics. — Member. 
Secretary. 

6 . Deputy Secretary, Department of Statistics. — Member, 
and assigns the following duties to the said committee, 
namely : — 

1. (a) preparation and submission to the Central Govern- 
ment of statements showing programmes of wort 
agreed to be undertaken bv the Indian Statistical 
Institute, Calcutta, during the year 1977-78 for 
which the Central Government may provide funds. 


as well as general financial estimates of such work; 
and 

(b) the settlement on broad fineB of the programme of 

work. 

2. The Committee shall submit its report by the 13th 
January, 1977. 

3. The Department of Statistics will perfoim the Secretariat 
functions of the Committee, the headquarters of which will 
be at New Delhi. 

[No. M-1201 1 /4/76-ISlj 
R. N. SEXANA, Dy. Secy.

New Delhi, the 6th August, 1976 
(INCOME-TAX) 

S.O. 3399. — It is hereby notified for general information 
that the institution mentioned below has been approved by 
the Secretary, Department of Science and Technology, New 
Delhi the prescribed authority for the purposes of clause (ii) 
of sub-section (1) of Section 35 of the Income-tax Act, 1961, 
subject to the fulfilment of the following conditions : — 

(i) That the Indian Institution of Plant Engineers, Madras 
will maintain a separate account of the sums received 
by it for scientific research. 

(ii) That the said Institution will furnish the Annual 
return of its Science Research activities to the pres- 
cribed authority for every financial year in such 
forms as may be laid down and Intimated to them 
for this purpose, by 30th April, each year. 

INSTITUTION 

THE INDIAN INSTITUTION OF PLANT ENGINEERS, 
MADRAS. 

This notification tak.es effect from 1st April, 1976,

S.O, 3400. — It is hereby notified for general Information 
that the institution mentioned below has been approved by 
Secretary, Department of Science and Technology, the pres- 
cribed authority for the purposes clause (ii) of Sub-section (1) 
of Section 35 of the Income-tax Act, 1961, subject to the 
fulfilment of the following conditions : — 

(i) That the Tamil Nadu Agricultural University, Coim- 
batore will maintain a separate account of the sums 
received by it for scientific research in the field 
of natural or applied sciences (other than Agricul- 
ture/Animal Husbandry /Fisheries and medicines); 

(ii) That the said University will furnish the annual 
return of its scientific research activities to the pres- 
cribed authority for every financial year in such 
forms as may be laid down and intimated to them 
for this purpose, by 30th April, each yeat,

(INCOME-TAX) 

S.O. 3401. — It is hereby notified for general information 
that the institution mentioned below has been approved by 
Secretary, Department of Science and Technology, ihe pres- 
cribed authority for the purposes of clause (ii) of sub- 
section (1) of Section 35 of the Income-tax Act, 1961, subject 
to the fulfilment of the following conditions : 

(i) That the Birbal Sahni Institute of Palaeobotany, 

Lucknow will maintain a separate account of the 
sums received by it for scientific research. 

(ii) That the said Institute will furnish the annual return 
of its scientific research activities to the prescribed 
authority for every financial year in such forms as 
may be laid down and intimated to them for 
this purpose, by 30th April, each year.

LUCKNOW. 

This notification takes effect from 19th June, 1976.

CENTRAL BOARD OF DIRECT TAXES 
New Delhi, the 15th July, 1976 

(INCOME-TAX) 

S.O.3413. . — In exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of Section 121 of the Income-tax Act, 1961 (43 
of 1961) and in partial modification of the previous notification 
of even number dated the 20th July, 1974, as amended from time 
to time by notifications of even number dated 30th April, 1975, 
20th May 1975 and 13th June 1975, the Central Board of Direct 
Tuxes, hereby direcl that the Commissioners of Income-tax 
specified in Column (1) of the Schedule hereto annexed with 
headquarters specified in Column (2) thereof, shall perform the 
functions in respect of such areas of such persons or classes of 
persons or of such incomes or classes of incomes or of such cases 
or classes of cases as arc comprised in the Income-tax Circles, 
Wards or Districts referred to in Column (3) : 

Provided that the Commissioner of Income-tax shall also 
perform his functions is respect of such persons or of such cases 
as have been or may hereafter he assigned by the Central Board 
of Direct Taxes to any Income-tax authority subordinate to 
him: 

Provided further that a Commissioner shall not perform his 
functions in respect of such persons or of such cases or as have 
been or may be assigned to any Income-tax authority outside 
his jurisdiction.

Control and Inspection) Rules, 1970, for rule 6 the follow-

rupees of F.O.B, value of each consignment shall 
be paid by the exporter to the Agency as inspection 

fee”

S.O. 3425 — In exercise of the powers conferred by sec- 
tion 17 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection) Act, 
1963 (22 of 1963), the Central Government hereby makes 
the following rules to amend the Export of Electric Cables 
and Conductors (Inspection) Rules, 196s, namely 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Export of Electric 
Cables and Conductors (Inspection) Amendment Rules, 1976. 

(2) They shall come Into force on the date of their publi- 
cation in the Official' Gazette. 

2. In the Export of Electric Cables and Conductors (Ins- 
pection) Rules, 1968, for rule 7, the following rule shall be 
substituted, namely : — 

“7. Inspection fee — Subject to a minimum of rupees ten, 
a fee at the rate of twenty paise for every hundred 
rupees of F.O.B. value of each consignment shall be 
paid by the exporter to the Agency as inspection fee 
for inspection of Electric Cables and Conductors. 
No separate inspection fee, however, shall required 
to be paid for such products as are already affixed 
with the recognised mark or seal in accordance 
with the provisions of the Indian Standards Institution 
(Certification Marks) Act, 1952 (36 of 1952), the 
Indian Standards Institution (Certification Marks) 
Rules, 1955 and the Indian Standards Institution 
(Certification Marks) Regulations, 1955”.

The Letters of Authority holdors of tho licences have intima- 
ted to this office that the Customs purposes copies and Exchange 
Control Copy of licences at SI. No. 2 together with Letter of 
Authority of tho above mentioned licences have been lost/mis- 
placed without having been registered at any Customs office 
and without opening Lotteis of Credits at all, In support of 
their contention they have filed affidavits. I am satislied that 
the original Exchange Control Copies & Customs purposes 
copies and Letters of Authority have been misplaced, 

In exercise of the powers conferred on me under Section 9 
(cc) Import (Control) Order, 1955, dated 7th December, 1955, 1 
order the cancellation of Exchange Control Copy and Customs 
purposes copies and Letters of Authority of the above mentioned 
licences. 

The applications arc now being considered for issue of 
Release Orders for a canalised item 'Sodium Cyanide' in lieu of 
above licences, as requested by the Firms, as per policy.

(Office of the Joint Chief Controller of Imports Si Exports) 
ORDER 

Madras, the 27th April, 1976 

S.O. 3433. — M/S. The Sarvodaya Industries, No, 6, Ambat- 
tur Industrial Estate, Ambattur, Madras-58, was issued an 
Import Licence No. P/S/8230330/C/XX/52/M/39 40/05/15, 
dated 30-9-1974 for Tin Plate Waste/Waste for Rs. 29,423 
under General Currency Area, for tho period 1974-75. They 
have applied for the issue of duplicate Customs Clearance 
Purposes copy of the above import licence on the giound that 
the same has been lost or misplaced without having been 
registered with any Customs Authority and utilised at all. 
In support of their contention, the applicant has filed an 
affidavit. 

I am satisfied that the original Customs Clearance Purposes 
copy of the Import Licence No. P/S/8230330/C/XX/52/ 
M/39-40/05/15, dated 30-9-1974 has been lost and direct 
that a duplicate Customs Clearance Purposes copy of import 
licence should be issued to the applicant. The original Cus- 
toms Clearance Purposes copy of the Import Licence No. P/ 
S/8230330/0/XX1/52/M/39-40/05/15, dated 30-9-1974 is 
hereby cancelled. 

[No. I&S/TP / 15 /MD/74-75] 
R. KUMARAVELU, Dy. Chief Controller

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 15th June, 1976 


S.O. 3434. — M/s. Times Surgical Co., 15/21, West Patel 
Nagar, Delhi were granted an Established Imports Licence 
No. P/E/0222889, dated 20-6-73 for Rs. 1250 only for import 
of Medical including Surgical Instruments Equiments and Ap- 
pliances Parts thereof not made mainly of Rubber and not 
made mainly of Glass as per conditions in the current Red 
Book Vol. I under I.T.C. S. No. 93-94(E) V. They have 
applied for the duplicate Exchange Control Copy of the 
licence on the ground that the original has been lost or mis- 
placed. It is further stated by the firm that the original Ex- 
change Control Copy of the licence was registered with Cus- 
toms House and has been fully utilised during the period 
A.M. 74. 

In support of this declaration, the applicant has filled an 
affidavit duly attested stating that the original Exchange Cont- 
rol Copy of the licence ha s been lost or misplaced. 

T am satisfied that the original Exchange Control Copy of 
the said licence has been lost and direct that the duplicate 
Exchange Control Copy of the said licence should be issued 
to the applicant. The original Exchange Control Copy of the 
licence is cancelled. 


The applicant is now being issued duplicate Exchange Copy 
of the aforesaid licence for Rs. 1250 only required by 
them for the purpose of utilisation of repeat operation against 
A.M. 75 licensing period. 

[File No. 93-94-V/42/AM-74/QL/CLA] 

D. P. MATHUR, Dy. Chief Controller, 
for Joint Chief Controller

MINISTRY OF INDUSTRY AND CIVIL SUPPLIES

INDIAN STANDARDS INSTITUTION 
New Delhi, 1976 September 7 

S.O, 3435. — In pursuance of sub-regulation (I) of Regulation 5 of the Indian Standards Institution (Certification Marks) 
Regulations, 1955, as amended from time to time, it is. herebv, notified that IS : 771 — 1963 Specification for glazed earthenware sanitary 
appliances (revised), details of which were published under notification number S.O, 1 102, dated 1964-03-18, in the Gazette of India, 
Part-11, Section-3, Sub-section (ii) dated 1964-03-28, has been withdrawn and stands cancelled as the glazed earthenware sanitary ap- 
pliance’s were outdated as well as unhygenic.

MINISTRY OF HEALTH & FAMILY PLANNING 
(Department of Health) 

New Delhi, the 7lh September, 1976 

S.O. 3437. — Whereas, in pursuance of clause (b) of sub- 
section (1) of section 3 of the Indian Medical Council Act, 
19J6 (102 of 1956). Dr. S. S. Gupta, Dean, Gandhi Medical 
College, Bhopal, has been elected by the Bhopal University 
to be a member of the Medical Council of India, with effect 
from the 22nd May, 1976. 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of sub-section (1) of Section 
3 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby makes the 
following further amendment in the notification of the Gov- 
ernment of India, late Ministry of Health, No. 5-13/59-Ml, 
dated the 9th January, 1960, namely : — 

In the said notification, under the heading “Elected under 
clause (b) of sub-section (1) of section 3”, against Serial No. 
48, for the entry, the following entry shall be substituted, 
namely : — 

“Dr. S, S. Gupta, 

Dean, 

Gandhi Medical College, 

Bhopal,"

New Delhi, the 9th September, 1976 

S.O. 3445 — Whereas a public notice for revising the local 
area of Billimora, Amalsad & Gandcvi Telephone Exchange 
systems was published as required by rule 434 (IU)(bb) of 
the Indian Telegraph Rules, 1951 in the Newspapers in 
circulation at Billimora. Amalsad and Gandevi inviting ob- 
jections and suggestions from all persons likely to be affected 
thereby, within a period of 30 days from the date of publica- 
tion of the notice in the Newspapers ; 

And whereas the said notice was made available to the 
public on 29th May, 1976 in the newspapers ‘Gujarat Mitra’ 

(Surat) and ‘Indian Express’ (Bombay); 

And whereas no objections and suggestions have been re- 
ceived from the public on the said notice; 

Now. therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
rule 434 (111) (bb) of the said Rules, the Director General 
Posts and Telegraphs hereby declares that with effect from 
1-10-76 the revised local area of Billimora, Amalsad & 

Gandevi shall be as under; 

Billimora Telephone Exchange System.— The local area of 
Billimora shall cover an urea falling under the jurisdiction of 
Billimora Municipality : 

Provided that the telephone subscribers located outside 
Billimora Municipal Limit but who arc served from Billimora 
Telephone Exchange System shall continue 10 pay local

tariffs as long as they are located within 5 Kms. of any ex- 
change of this system and remain connected to it. 

Amalsad Telephone Exchange System. — The local aica of 
Amnlsad shall cover an men falling within 5 Kms, radial 
distance from Amalsad 'telephone Exchange: 

Piovided that this limit shall be restricted to Anrbikn River 
in East and South-East. 

Gandevi Telephone Exchange System.— The local area of 
Gandevi shall cover an area falling within 5 Kms. radial dis- 
tance from Gandevi Telephone Exchange : 

Provided that this limit shall be lcxtijcted to Ambihn 
River in the West and to the Southern boundary of villages 
Devsnr, Khaisam and Thcnoii in the South.

S.O. 3446. — Whereas a public notice for revising the local 
aica of Patna and Danapur Telephone Exchange System was 
published as rcquiied by rule 434 ( 1 1 1 ) ( bb > of rhe Indian 
Telegraph Rules, 1951 in the Newspapers in circulation at 
Patna & Danapur inviting objections and suggestions fiom all 
persons likely to he affected thereby, wiltiin a period of 
30 days from the date of publication of the notice in the 
Newspapers ; 

11 GI/76 — 9 


And whereas the said notice was made available to the 
public oil Uth July, 197n in Neuxpupei s ‘Indian Nation’, 
‘Seaichlight’, ‘Aiyavarla’, ’Pradccp' and ‘Satlii’ Patna ; 

And whereas no objections and suggestions have been re- 
ceived from ihe public on the said notice ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by i ule 
434((II)(bb) of the said Rules, the Dilector Gcncial Posls 
and felegiaphs heicby declares that with effect from 1 -JO-76 
the revised local area of Patna and Danapui shall be as 
under : — 

Patna Telephone Exchange System.— The local urea of 
Patna shall cover an area Jailing under Ihe jurisdiction of 
Patna Municipality : 

Provided that the telephone subsciibcis located outside 
Patna Municipal limit but are served from Patna Telephone 
Exchange System shad continue to pay local taiiffs as long 
as they are located within 5 Kins, of any exchange of this 
system and lcmain connected to it, 

Danapur Telephone Exchange System. — The local area of 
Danapur shall cover an area falling under the jurisdiction 
of Danapur Municipality : 

Provided that the telephone subscribers located outside Dana- 
pur Municipal’ limit but who aie scived from Danapur Tele- 
phone Exchange System shall continue to pay local tariffs 
as long as they arc located within 5 Kms. of any exchange 
of this system and remain connected to it.

tervicc of their employee, Shri Rolstan John 
Mechunic-cum-opcraloi, Oiadc ill Mnjhagawnn with

2. It is rtot disputed that Shii Roldan John was a perma- 
nent employee ol standing. He went on sanctioned leave 
from 23-7-74 to 29 7-74. He overstayed and was ultimately 
informed that his service stood teimmated with effect from 
10-8 l y 74. He was not allowed to work .since then. After 
representations made to the management and after giving legal 
notice attain'd (he said stoppage of work which according 
to him amounted (o illegal termination of scivice, he refened 
the mailer to the Assistant I abour Commissioner (Central). 

3 The case of (he workman is that when he was on leave 
a t Delhi his mother fell seriously ill and there was none else 
to look after her, hence he sent an application on 2-8-1974 
for extension of leave upto 6-8-1974. The extension was 
sanctioned and he joined hack his duties on 7th August, 1974. 
On 8th also be worked upto 2.30 p.m. when he was infoimcd 
that he should see Executive Engineer (M). The said officer 
was not available either on 8th or on 91h when ultimately he 
was informed on 10th August, 1974 that he shall not be taken 
(sack on duty as the extension of his leave was not (-auc- 
tioned. The management was biased. Jt’s previous allcmpi to 
similarly terminate his teiviccs had been frustrated and that 
had caused further annoyance against him. 

4. Management lilt'd t he preliminary objection against vali- 
dity and maintainability of the reference denying that he 
returned to duty within 10 days of the expiry of leave origi- 
nally granted or explained to (he satisfaction of the manage- 
ment his inability to so rclurned to duty after the expiry of 
the said leave. I his resulted in automatic termination of ser- 
vice under C ! ausc 24(c) of Certified Standing Orders. As 
in view of the above circumstances the management had rut 
terminated his service, S, 2-A of Industrial Disputes Acl dkl 
not apply and the workman had no right to raise an industrial 
dispute. The reference was thus wrongly made and calls 
for no reply by this Tiibunal. 

5. Clause 24(c) of the Standing Orders runs as follows : 

“If a workman remains absqnl beyond the period of leave 
originally granted or subsequently extended he shall 
lo'e lien on his appointment unless — 

(i) he returns within 10 days of expiry of his leave ; 
and 

(il) explains to h 'itisfaction of (he management his 
innbilhy to return on the expiry of his leave"’ 

6. The letter of the management Tlx. W/34 indicates that 
according to Clause 33(xxvi) of Standing Orders overstaying 
the sanctioned leave amounts to r,-fi , conduct. Thus Clause 24 fe) 
contemplates automatic termination for which neither any 
notice nor any enquiry is necessary while Clause 33(x:tvi) 
biings it under the definition of misconduct lor which the 
employee can be charge-sheeted & removed from scivice after 
due cnquiiy. In National Engineering Industry J.td. Vs. H aim- 
man (2) S.C.L.l. 1037) para 13, in similar circumstances of 
existence of two such provisions in tlw Si muling Order it 
was held Ihat : 

“In our opinion (the existence of the alternate provision 
declaring the absence as misdemeanour) is an alter- 
nate provision and the appellant in this case was 
free to resort to any ond of the provisions, unless it 
is shown Ihnf resort to one particular provision was 
due to malafides.” 

7. Tn the present case also the workman has raised allega- 
tions of malafides and victimisation. Therefore the question 
whether the management was justified in not resorting to the 
regular enquiry for misconduct of absence for more than It) 
days beyond fhe period of sanctioned leave, will depend upon 
the proof or disproof of it’s nwInfidS intention in hiking the 
action. 

8. The workman specifically alleged in paras 6 and 7 of 
the written statement that only about 4 months back the 
management had charge-sheeted him for overstaying the sanc- 
tioned leave in April 1974 when lie had telegraphically applied 
for extension and the management had sanctioned the same 


9. The workman further alleged that he had this time 
sem by post an application on 2-8-74 for extension of leave 
suppotled by a medical certificate. That extension of leave 
was sanctioned and on t he expiry of such extended leave lie 
joined bis duties on 7-8-76. The management has obliquely 
denied the'e allegations in the later part of pain 2 of its 
written statement but has failed to produce any evidence in 
rebuttal to the one produced by the workman on this point. 
The workman examined himself on oath and produced cei- 
tain documentary evidence. His statement on oath fully 
supports the aforesaid allegations made by him in his written 
statement. The application for leave was sent under certificate 
of posting. The certificate is Fx, W/33. Tt hears the postal 
seal of 2nd of August, 1974. The woiknun, W.W.l, has 
stated on oalh that the application for extension of leave 
(Copy Ex. W 32) was sent under that postal certificate. Once 
the posting of a letter is proved, it will be presumed that the 
same was delivered to the addressee in due comse The 
addressee Executive Engineer, N.M.D.C. Majhgavan Mines or 
any other person on his behalf has not appeared in the 
witness box to deny the receipt of such a letter On the other 
hand, the photostat copy (original was with the workman) 
l x. W/24 dated 10-8-1974 indicates that the question of ex- 
tension of leave was till 9-8-1974 pending with the manage- 
ment. This means that the management had received the 
application for extension and that was under consideration 
on 9-8-74. There is no reason to disbelieve (his evidence. 
The letter Ex. W/24 shows that mines manager gave that 
written note on the persuation of Messrs D. P. Singh M V. R 
Rao and other executive of M.P.R.H.K.S. Union Majhgavan, 
1 his recital in the note is also significant. It means that the 
management was otherwise reluctant to commit anything in 
writing. Tt speaks against the bortafide r and the intentions be- 
come much more clear when the management comes 
beloie. this Court suppressing the application for 

extension of leave and falsely denying all facts about 
such extension or joining of duty on 7-8-1976 by the work- 
man. There is no straight plea that the said leave application 
was rejected. This was deliberate because it would have 
raised questions as to how & why and under what circums- 
tances it was so rejected and why the previous precedent of 
treating it as misconduct and charge sheeting the workman 
wasl not followed this time. It is the settled law that bonafdn 
°t the management are in question the Tribunal can tear 
out tho veil and look into the back ground of the otherwise 
innocuous looking termination simplicitor or automatic Such 
Power was conceded to the Tribunal in L. Michiel It another 
Vs. Johnson Pumps (1975-I-LLJ 262 SC). Bclicvinn this 
uni emitted evidence I am of the view that the action on tile 
part ol (he management was malafide. 

10. Now as the action was malafide (he management was 
du y bound to explain why it chose an action under Clause 
246:1 treating the absence as amounting to automatic termi- 
nation as against charge sheeting (he workman for the mw- 
vondncl- of such absence The a.lwser appears to be obvious. 
Domestic enquiry would have given an opportunity to the 
woikman to show cause and he had sufficient evidence to 
shut (he month! of falncies. The management would not have 
succeeded in its efforts to terminate the services of this 
r, r , m an : n i hat case " Under t,lc circumstances it is obvious

Tn the piesent case the rejection of application foi extension of

14 Automatic teimlnntion urclei Clause 24(c) of Standing

15. The i ejection of the application for extension of leave 
would have the effect of snapping the lien to the nppointmtni 
hence such snapping is not automatic but in pcisuunce of (he

16. Such tcimination amounts to termination by way of 
retrenchment and would be void because retrenchment com-

17 Thus the facts of the case make it dc.ii that the

else " 

Thus it k clear that, there was Industrial dispute, lcfeiencc was

HARBANS BAHADUR, Section Officer (Spl.) 

New Delhi, the 10th September, 1976 

S.O. 3450. — Tn pursuance of section 17 ol' the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hcieby publishes the following award of the Central Gov- 
ernment Industrial Tribunal, Delhi in the industrial dispute 
between the employers in relation to the management of the 
Delhi Milk Scheme and their workmen, which was received 
by the Central Government on the 6th September, 1976. 

BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CFNTRAL GOVT. 

INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, DELHI 
C.G.I.D. No. 27 of 1975 
BETWEEN 

The management of M/s. Delhi Milk Scheme, West 
Patel Nagar, New Dclhi-8. 

AND 

Its workmen S/Shu Sidhu Ram and Prabhu Dayal ns 
represented by Central Dairy Mazdoor Sangh, C-98, 
New' Moti Nagar, New Delhi. 

Shi i D. S. Adel — for Ihe Management, 

Shri Shukhla — for the Workman. 

AWARD 

The Central Government on consideration of a report 
submitted by the Conciliation Officer that an industrial dis- 
pute existed between the aforesaid paities has referred the 
same for adjudication to this Tribunal by its Order No. L- 
42012(49)/74-l.R. Ill/D. 2(B) dated the '1st July, 1975 wilh 
the following terms of reference : — 

“Is the management of Delhi Milk Scheme, New Delhi 
justified in not tiunsfcrring Sarvashri Sidhu Ram 
and Prabhu D.iyal, Carpenter and Mason lcspec- 
tivcly, to the regular establishment when they have 
become entitled to it, in accordance with the Certi- 
fied Standing Orders and in terminating their ser- 
vices w'ilh effect from 13-9-73 ? if not, to what 
relief aie the said workman entitled ?” 

2. This reference is in respect of two persons S/Shri Sidhu 
Ram and Prabhu Dayal. Their case was that they were em- 
ployed as casual workers to the posts of carpenter and mason 
respectively. They w'erc not transferred to regular establish- 
ment even though they had continuously worked for Ihree 
months in regular work. It was, also, pleaded in the alternative 
that even if if was presumed that the two workmen were not 
casual workers they were Badli workers and as such they 
should have been transferred to regular establishment as 
they had actually worked for not less than 240 days in a 
penod of 12 months. It was further pleaded that their 
services were terminated. The applicants called this action 
of the management bad, unfair, influenced by anti-labour 
practice and without jurisdiction and prayed that an award 
directing the management to transfer them to the regular estab- 
lishment be pussed, as also, their termination be declared un- 
justified and they be re-instated. 

3. The management stated that the workman could not 
bo transferred to regular establishment as they did not fulfil 
the condition laid down in clause (2) and clause (3) of Order 
4 of (ho certified Standing Orders, ft was further stated that 
since they did not satisfy the requirements of the recruit- 
ment rules they could not he appointed. It was prayed that 
reference be merited dismissal. 


1. As in te term of reference 

5. In oral evidence the management examined Shri R. R. 
Mittal MWI. The workman did not adduce any evidence on 
their side. 

Issue No.-l 

6. The first question for determining under the teim of 
reference is whether the action of the management in not 
transferring the workmen, herein, to regular establishment 
was justified. 

7. The case of the workman is that they weic casual 
workers. They worked continuously for (hi ce months in regu- 
lar work, They were, thercfoie, entitled to be transferred to 
lcgular establishment under clause (2) of Order 4 of the 
Standing Orders. 

R. The defence of the management is that the workmen, 
herein, did not work for thiec months in regular woik. They 
were, therefore, not entitled to be transferred to regular 
establishment. 

9. The burden of proving that the workmen worked con- 
tinuously for thiec months in tegular work lay on them. 
They did not give any evidence in support of it . II was. there- 
fore, not proved that they worked continuously for three 
months in regular work, that is to say, they failed to show 
that they worked continuously for three months other than 
as casual worker or against the regular work. In failure of 
such evidence, therefore, they could not be, held to be en- 
titled to be transferred lo the regular establishment. 

10. The workmen then contended in the alternative that 
they were Badli Workers and having worked for 240 days 
in a year they were entitled to be transfeived to a regular 
establishment, Now, firstly, this case is at variance wilh the 
pleading that they were appointed casual workers. Besides 
this there was no evidence to the effect that they were Badli 
workers or that they actually worked for 240 days, us such, 
on this basis also they failed to prove that they were entitled 
to be transferred to regular establishment, 

11. The total result is that the workmen failed to prove 
that they were entitled to be transferred to regular establish- 
ment. This part of the reference is, therefore, decided against 
the workmen. 

12. The second part of the reference is that the services 
of the workmen were terminated with effect from 13-9-73. 
The burden of proving this, also, lay on the workmen. Tt 
seems that the workmen did not press this part at ail as no 
evidence was load about it nor was anything said about it 
at the time of arguments. This part is, also, therefore, decided 
against the workmen, 

13. The folal result is that the management has succeeded 
in showing that its action was juslified and the workmen have 
failed to show that 1 he action of the management was un- 
justified. The workmen, heroin, are, therefore, not entitled 
to any relief. An award is made accordingly. 

D. D. GUPTA, Presiding Officer 
[No. L-42012/49/74-LR. Ill/D. 11(B)] 

26th August, 1976,

5.0. 3452.— In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern- 
ment ’ Industrial Tribunal, Delhi in the industrial dispute 
between the employers in relation to the management of the 
Union Bank of India, Meerut and their workmen, which 
was received by the Central Government on the 6-9-76. 

BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL 

GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL DELHI 

C.G.l.D. No. 43 of 1975 

BETWEEN 

The General Manager, Union Bank of India, H, O. 
Bombay. 

AND 

Its workman us represented by U. P. Bank Employees 
Union, Saharanpur (The Union) through its 
Secretary. 

PRESENT 

Shri S. S. Sethi for the management. 

Shii T. C. Garg for ihe workman. 


AWARD 

‘I he Central Government on being satisfied that an indus- 
tiial dispute existed between ihe aforesaid parties has referr- 
ed ihe same for adjudication to this Tribunal by its ordet 
No. L- 1 2012/58 /75/DI I /A, dated the 22nd July, 1975 with 
the following terms of reference : 

“Whether the management of the Union Bank of India, 
Regional Office, Meerut is justified in not absorbing 
Shri Suresh Chand lemporary Godown-kcepcr 
Saharanpur in the permanent service of the Bank V 
Tf not, to what relief is the said wokroati entitled ? 

2. The case of the wokman was 1 h at he was appointed ns 
Ciodown-keeper by the Dy. General Manager and posted at 
Etawah, on 28th March, 1972. The appointment was termi- 
nable on expiry of 3 months. As the work was of permanent 
nutnie the workman had to be again employed and directed to 
again join on 1-7-72. It was staled thut hq was illegally termina- 
ted on 6-2-73 and in his place a Godown-kceper-cum-Ca shier 
was posted the Etawah : JTe w'ns again, called back on 30-5-73 
and posted at Saharanpur Branch of the Rank, He was laid off 
several times during his employment. It was pleaded that the 
nominal lay-offs termed as break in service by the Bank from 
lanuary 1975 onward were in utter violation of Section 33-A 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. ft vvas further pleaded 
that the Bank had illegally deducted the full wages Tor the 
period of lay-offs and also for lire closed days prefixed and 
or suffixed to the period actually worked by the workman. 
It was, therefore, prayed that the workman was entitled to be 
declared as a permanent confirmed employee of the Bank on 
expiry of six months period of probation from his original 
appointment on 28-3-72 and was entitled to be treated as 
Godown-kccper-cum-Clcark to get full wages and due incre- 
ments. 

3. The respondent raised the preliminary objections that 
Ihe dispute had not been validly espoused by the U.P. Bank 
Employees Union, Saharanpur or a substantial number oT 
woikman. On merits it was slated that employment of a 
Godov. n-keeper was of temporary nature, It was further 
stated that the workman was employed on temporary basis 
till 27th June, 1972 when his services automatically came to 
an end as per ihe terms of Ihe employmept, It was, therefore, 
prayed that the workman was not entitled to any relief. 

4. The workman filed a rejoinder and reiterated his cluim, 

5. On these pleadings the following three issues were 
framed : — 

ISSUES : 

1. Whether the dispute has been validly and properly 

espoused; if not, its effect. 

2. As in the terms of reference. 

3. Relief. 

6. The parties did not adduce any oral evidence, 

7. Arguments were, then, heard. 

ISSUE NO : 1 

8. The question for determination ts whether the cause of 
Ihe workman, herein, was validly and properly espoused. The 
espousal was needed for the reason that the reference did not 
fall under Section 2-A of the Industrial Disputes Act. 

9. The .burden of proving lhat theic was espousal lay on 
the workman. He, however, did not adduce any evidence to 
show that bis cause espoused by a substantial number of 
woikman of the respondent. 

10. The result is that espousal not having been proved, the 
issue is decided against him. 

11. Since there is no espousal there is no industrial dis- 
pute, either, between the parties. This reference is, therefore, 
incompetent. An award is made accordingly, 

17th August, 1976 

D. D. GUPTA, Presiding Officer.

ber 1977. 

2. The above exemption is subject to the following condi- 
tions, namely : — 

(1) The employer of the said factory shall submit in respect 
of the period during which that factory was subject to the 
operation of the said Act (hei einaftcr leferred to as the said 
pciiod), such returns in such form and containing such parti-

(i) verifying the particulars contained in any return 

submitted under sub-section (1) of section 44 for 
the said period; or 

(ii) ascertaining whether registeis and records were main- 

tained as required by the Employees’ State Insurance 
(General) Regulations, 1950 for the said period; 
or 

(iii) ascertaining whcthci the employees continue to he 

entitled to benefits piovided by the employer in 
cash and hind beinR benefits in consideration of 
which exemption is being granted under this noti- 
fication; or 

(iv) ascertaining whether any of the provisions of the 

Act had been complied with during the period when 
such provisions were in forco in relation to the said 
factory; 

be empowered to— 

(a) requiic the piincipal or immediate employer to fur- 

nish to him such information as he may consider 
necessary; or

miscs occupied by such principal or immediate em- 
ployer at any teasonable time and requiic any

loyee; or

S.O. 3457.— Whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
that tho employees of the Oie Handling Plunt Site Workshop. 
Visakhapatnain Poit Tuist, Visakhapptnam, are otherwise in 
icceipt of benefits substantially similar to the benefits provided 
tinder the pmployees' State Insuiunce Act, 1948 (14 of 1948); 

Now, theiefoie, in exercise of the powers conferred by sec- 
tion 90 of the said Act, the Central Government, after com 
saltation with the Employees’ State Insurance Corporation, 
heieby exempts the above montioned factory from the opera- 
tion of the said Act for a period of one year from the date 
of publication of this notification in the Official Gazette. 

2. The above exeniplion is subject to the following condi- 
tions, namely ; — 

(1) The employer of the said factory shall submit in res- 
pect of the period during which that factory was subject 
to the operation of the said Act (hereinafter referred to as 
the said period), such returns in such form and containing 
such particulars as were due from it in respect of the said 
period under the Employees’ Slate .Insurance (General) 
Regulations, 1950 ; 

(2) Any Inspector appointed by the Corporation under sub- 
section (.1) of section 45 of the said Act, or other Official 
of the Corporation authorised in this behalf shall, for the 
purposes of — 

(i) verifying the pmticulars contained in any return sub- 

mitted under sub-section (1) of section 44 for the 
said period ; or 

(ii) ascertaining whether registers and records were main- 

tained as required by the Employees’ Slate Insurance 
(General) Regulations, 1950 for the said period ; or 

(lii) ascertaining whether the employees continue to be 
entitled to benefits provided by the employer in 
cash and kind being boneflts in consideration of which 
exemption is being granted under this notification ; or 

(iv) ascertaining whether any of (he provisions of the Act 
had been complied with during the period when such 
provisions were in force in relation to the said 
factory ; 

he empowered to — 

(a) require the principal or immediate employer to fur- 
nish to him such information as he may consider 

necessary ; or

such accounts, books and other documents relating 
to the employment of persons and payment of wages 
or to furnish to him such information as he may 
consider necessary ; or 

(c) examine the principal or immediate employer, his 
agent or servant, or any person found in such fac- 
tory, establishment, office or other premises, 01 any 
person whom the said Inspector or other official has 
reasonable cause to believe to have been an 
employee ; or 

fd) make copies of or take extracts from, any register, 
account book or other document maintained in such 
factory, establishment, office or other premises.

EMPLOYEES’ STATE INSURANCE CORPORATION 

REVISED ESTIMATES FOR THE YEAR 1975-76 
& BUDGET ESTIMATES FOR THE YEAR 1976-77 

At their mettings held on the 18th and 19th February, 1975, 
the Standing Comittee and the Corporation approved the Budget 
Estimates of the Receipts and Expenditure of the Employees’ 
State Insurance Corporation for the financial year 1975-76. 
These were approved by the Central Government vide Ministry 
of Labour letter No. G. 20017(3)/75-Hl dated the 8th April, 1975. 

2. The Budget Estimates approved by the Central Govern- 
ment covered 

(i) measures needed for the running of the scheme in various 
centres where it has already been implemented ;

(ii) measures needed for and the extension of the scheme to 
new areas. 

3. When thc~Budget Estimates for 1975-76 were framed, 
It was anticipated that (t) the Scheme would be extended to new 
areas and (ii) medical care would be extended to the families of the 
Insured Persons as per programme detailed in Appendix-I 
from the dates shown against each in columns 3 and 5 thereof. 
Howervor, )duo to administrative and other difficulties in 
making adequate medical arrangements by Ihe State Govern- 
ments concerned, the programme of implementation and exten- 
sion of medical care to the families had to be modified. The 
Scheme was actually extended to some of the areas from dates 
iater than those originally planned as per details shown in Column 
4 of Appendix-I. As regards the areas where the expectation to 
extend the Scheme could not materialise, the revised dates, as 
f now anticipated, have boen indicated against each item in appro- 
priate columns of the above referred Appendix. The dates from 
which the Medical Care has been or is likely to be extended to 
families have also been indicated in column 6 of Appendix-I. 

4. As a result of further discussion and correspondence with 
the various State Governments. It is now anticipated that the 
Scheme will implemented in new areas duling the financial year 
1975-76 and 1976-77 from the dates as shown in Appendix-IL 
For the sake of convenience, the places where the Scheme has 
already boen implemented in the past with dates of implementa- 
tion have also been indicated in this Appendix. The number 
of employees already covered or proposed to be covered has also 
been revised in the light of latest information available and incor- 
orted in Appendix-IL— The dates from which the medical care 
as been extended or is likely to be extended to the familios of 
insured persons have also been indicated against each item in 
this Appendix. 

5. The Revised Estimates for the financial year 1975-76 
and the Budget Estimates for the year 1976-77 have been prepared 
in the light of the revised programme of implementation.

actuals of receipts/oxpenditurc for the past three years and 
Revised Estimates for 1975-76 and Estimates for 1976-77 are 
submitted for consideration and approval.

(b) The transactions shown under the heading .Headquarters’ 
in the above statements also include expenditure on certain 
centralised items of expenditure incurred in respect of Regional 
and Local Offices e.g., contributions to the Provident Fund of the 
employees of the Corporation and Pension Reserve Fund, Leave 
and Pension Contribution payable in respect of Government 
servants on deputation in the Corporation, publicity, expendi- 
ture on contribution stamps, audit fees etc, 

8, Contributions 

Employers’ and Employees’ shares of contribution are payable 
through a single contribution stamp as per Schedule-1 of the ESI 
Act, 1948 as modified by the E.S.I. Amendment Act, 1975. The 
amendment took effect from 30-11-1975. An annual increase of 
30% in revenue on account of rationalisation of contribution 
rates and additional coverage of employees getting wages above 
Rs. 500 and upto Rs. 1000/- has been provided for in the esti- 
mates. 

9. Medical Benefits 

(a) The expenditure under the head "A-Mcdical Benefits”, 
except for the Union Territory of Delhi where the Scheme is 
directly administered by the Corporation, is initially incurred by 
the State Governments and is later shared between the Corpora- 
tion and the State Governments in the prescribed ratio of 7 : 1. 
The provision made under this head is intended to cover the Cor- 
poration’s share of the expenditure. 

(b) Ccllling on Expenditure on Medical Benefits. 

The celling of yearly shareable expenditure on medical bene- 
fits per employee have been revised as follows with effect from 
1st April, 1975 — 

(i) From Rs, 65 to 70 in the areas where restricted medical 
cure is provided. 

('if) From Rs. 70 to Rs. 75 in areas where expended medical 
care Is provided. 

(iii) From Rs. 85 to Rs, 95 in ureas where full medical care 
is provided. 

(cf Payments to State Governments. 

The Corporation makes during the year ’on account’ pay- 
ments ro the extent of about 90% of its share of expenditure on 
medical benefits, on the basis of expenditure statements received 
from the State Governments subject to adjustments on receipt 
of audit certificates from he concerned State Accountant General. 

(d) F.xiocnses incurred directly by the Corporation. 

The provision made under the head ‘‘Modical treatment and 
care and maternity facilities, (expenses incurred directly by the 
Corporation)”, includes the estimated cost of administration of 
the Medical care to the Insured Persons and their families in the 
Union Territory of Delhi, taken over by the Corporation with 
effect from 1st April, 1962. The anticipated recovery at the rate 
of l/8th ot shareable amount has been taken into account in the 
Revised Estimates 1975-76 and Budget Estimates 1976-77 
on the Revenue side under the head "State Govcmment’s/Union 
Territories share towards medical benefits initially incurred by 
the Corporation’’. 

REVISED ESTIMATES FOR THE YEAR 1975-76 
RECEIPTS 

10. The Revenue of the Corporation for the current year 
1975-76 is now estimated at Rs. 8,120.09 lacs as against Rs. 
8,004.74 lacc assumed in the Budget. There is therefore an 
increase of Rs. 115,35 lacs. 

Interest and Dividends 

Receipts on account of interest and dividends from the 
investment of surplus Cash Balances and interest on advances 
granted 1o Corporation employees for the purchase of con- 
veyances, house building purposes etc. during 1975-76 arc ex- 
ected to be Rs, 429.52 lacs against the Budget Estimates of Rs, 
64.39 lues. The increase is mainly due to larger sums abailable 
for investments and higher rates of interest etc, 

Rent of Hospital and Dispensary 

Buildings owned by the Corporation 

The income of the Corporation from rent of ESI Hospital 
and dispensary buildings together with the residential staff 


quarters attached thereto is anticipated to be Rs. 189.64 lacs, 
The rent so recovered will form a part of the shareable expenditure 
incurred by the State Government s on the provision of medical 
benefits to the Insured Persons and would, thus, be automatically 
apportioned between the Corporation and the State Government 
in the prescribed ratio of 7 ; L 

EXPENDITURE 

1 1 . The expenditure on Renevue Account in (he current year 
1975-76 is now estimated to be Rs. 7736.46 lacs as against Rs. 
7321.93 lacs anticipated in the Budget. 

12. Head-T-Bcneflts to Insured Persons and their families. 

A — MEDICAL BENEFITS 

Subject to the contents of para 9(c) the total provison under 
this head is Rs, 3366 . 61 lacs which comprises of Rs. 3 191 . 97 lacs 
as Corporation’s share of expenditure on medical treatment and 
equipment for hospitals ; Rs. 164.64 lacs as expenditure on 
Medical Benefits in Delhi where the Scheme is directly adminis- 
tered by the Corporation and Rs. 10 lacs toward s the payment 
of confinement lees payable under the Scheme to the bene- 
ficiaries in Maharashtra Region. In respect of Delhi, the recovery 
ol l/8(h expenditure have been taken into account on the receipt 
side of the Budget in 1976-77, The l/8th share of confinement 
charges due from Maharashtra Slate will be adjusted while reimb- 
bursing their icaim for expenditure on medical benefits. 

B— CASH BENEFITS AND C — OTFIER BENEFITS 

13. The Revised Provision under Cash Benefits now stands at 
Rs. 2557. 13 lacs as compared to Rs, 2222 63 lacs anticipated in 
the Budget. 

A provision of Rs. 8. 18 lacs has been made in the Revised 
Estimates against Budget Estimates of Rs. 9.39 lacs under C— 
Other Benefits. 

14. Head-2 — Administration Expenses. 

(a) The total Administration expenses during the year 1975- 
76 are anticipated to be Rs. 876.43 lacs as against Rs. 756.74 
lacs originally provided for in the Budget Estimates. 

(b) The provision for Administration Expenses has been made 
on the actuals for the first 8 months of the current year 1975-76 
and the anticipated requirements for the remaining four months. 

(c) The per capita administrative expenditure as per the 
Revised Estimated comes to Rs. 18.77 per insured employee 
per annum, The increase is mainly due to grant of additional 
instalments of dearness allowance to employees. 

The contingent charges have mainly increased due to pay- 
ments to be made to India Security Press, Nasik for purchase of 
paper and printing of coniribution stamps and levy of tax on 
ESI Projects retrospectively by the State Government of Tamil 
Nadu. Besides the above, additional staff had to be provided 
on account of extension of coverage to new sectors and increase 
of wage limits for coverage above Rs. 500/- and upto Rs. 1,000/- 
Provisioa of essential articles of stationery and forms, furniture, 
equipment for records, rent etc. have also been made on this 
account. 

Ufa) HEAD — 3-— HOSPITALS/DISPENSARIES 

The pi ovision under this head comprises of (i) Deprecia- 
tion of Hospital/Dispensary buildings (Rs. 24.00 lacs) (ii) 
Repair and Maintenance of these buildings (Rs. 70.00 lacs). 

(b) Contributions to Capital Construction and Emergency Re- 
set ve Fund 

EMERGENCY RESERVE FUND 

As decided by the Corporation in their meeting held on 17th 
March, 1973, 20% of the excess of income over expenditure sub- 
ject io'a minimum of Rs. one crore and whole of the excess if 
it is loss than one crore is to be credited to “Emergency Reserve 
Fund’’. Accordingly provision of Rs. 100 lacs and Rs, 285.67 
lacs have been made in the Revised Estimates for the current 
year and Budget Estimates for the next year respectively. 

(c) CAPITAL CONSTRUCTION RESERVE FUND. 

As in the previous years, 10% of the total Employers and

16. EXPENDITURE ON CAPITAL ACCOUNT 

The amount originally provided for expenditure on Capital 
Account was Rs. 748.17 lacs comprising of (i) Rs. 65 lacs for 
the construction of office buildings (Including staff quarters) 

(ii) Rs. 681.63 lacs for the construction of hospitals and dispen- 
saries and (iii) Rs. 2.22 lacs for the purchase of staff cars (in- 
cluding Rs. 68,000 front Depreciation Reserve Fund). 

The provision for Rs. 801 . 25 lacs has been made in the Re- 
vised Estimates 1975-76 as follows : — 


(a) Office Buildings (Including Staff Quarters) 

The provision of Rs. 65 lacs made in Ihe Budget Estimates 
for 1975-76 has been enhanced to Rs. 106.14 lacs in Revised 
Estimates for 1975-76. This is based on the trend of actuals 
and anticipated payments, 

(b) Buildings of Hospitals & Dispensaries 

The provision of Rs. 681.63 lacs under tins head has been 
enhanced to Rs. 693.43 lacs in the Revised Estimates for 1975-76 
on the basis of trend of actuals and anticipated payments. 

(c) Staff Cars. 

A provision of Rs. 1 . 68 lacs has been made for the purchase 
of staff cars in the Revised Estimates 1975-76, against Rs. 2.22 
lacs provided in the Budget Estimates 1975-76. 

17. LOANS TO STATE GOVERNMENTS 


The Government of Maharashtra approached the Cotpora- 
tion for expansion of ESI Projects built oul of loans already paid 
by the Corporation. As the expansion at the sole cost of the Cor- 
poration would mean joint ownership which involved complica- 
tions, it wati decided to agree to further loans to State Govern- 
ment for expansion of such ESI Projects. Accordingly, provision 
for loans for Rs. 23,58,590 in Revised Estimates for 1975-76 and 
Rs.4,56,500 in Budget Estimates for 1976-77 have been made.

19, BUDGET ESTIMATES FOR THE YEAR 1976-77 
RECEIPTS 

(a) Income on account of contributions (Employers’ 
and Employees’ Shares) has been estimated as Rs. 9845.82 lacs 
in accordance with the rates prescribed in Schedule-T of the ESI 
Act, 1948 modified by ESI Amendment Act, 1975. 

(b) State Government Shares towards Modical Benefits initially 
incurred by the Corporation 

An amount of Rs. 20.58 lacs has been included in the Budget 
Estimates for the year 1976-77 under the head “State Govern- 
ments'/Union Terrilories' share towards medical Benefits ini- 
tially incurred by the Corporation”. This represents recovery 


of the State Governments’ share of the expenditure likely to be 
incurred by the Corporation on Ihe administration of medical 
care lo the Insured Persons and their families in Delhi during 

1975-76, 

(c) Interest & Dividends 

Receipts on account of interest and dividends from the invest- 
ments of surplus cash balance and interest on advance granted 
to Corporation employees for the purchase of conveyances, 
house building purposes etc. are expected to be Rs. 472 . 59 
lacs as against the Revised Estimates of Rs. 429.52 lacs. 

(d) Rent of Hospital & Dispensaries Buildings owned by the 
Corporation 

A sum of Rs. 215.41 lacs is expected to be recovered from 
the State Governments on account of rent of the Hospital and 
dispensary buildings owned by the Corporation. 

20. EXPENDITURE 

Tho increased provision under the various heads in the Budget 
Estimates for the year 1976-77 as compared to corresponding 
provision in the Revised Estimates for the year 1975-76 is mainly 
duo to 

(i) The extension of medical care to the families to insured 
persons; 

(ii) the extension of tho Scheme to new areas. 

(iii) Ihe operation of the Scheme in areas whore the imple- 
mentation was brought about during the year 1974-75 
for full year, and 

(iv) expected increase in employment and wages in the im- 
plemented areas. 

21. A— MEDICAL BENEFITS 

A total provision of Rs. 3932.06 lacs has been made in the 
Budget Estimates 1976-77 for medical benefits on the basis of 
assumed number of employees as on 1-4-1976 which has been 
estimated 6% higher than the figures as on 31-3-1975 as detailed 
in Appendix-II taking’into account the extension of tho scheme 
and to provide for the growth in employment in implemented 
areas. It includes Rs. 197.57 lacs to be incurred directly by the 
Corporation during 1976-77 for providing medical care to the 
Insured Persons and their families in ihe Union Territory of 
Delhi and also Rs. 10 lacs to bo spent directly by the Corporation 
towards payment of confinement fees in the State of Maharash- 
tra. l/8th of the latter (Rs. 10 lacs) will be deducted from the 
claim of the Stale Government for the reimbursement of expens- 
es incurred by it on the provision of medical benefits to the In- 
sured Persons in the State. 

22. B— CASH BENEFITS & C— OTHER BENEFITS 

Provisions made for the various cash benefits and other bene- 
fits is mainly based on the actuals for ihe first 8 months of the 
year 1975-76. The impact of the proposed 25% incrcaso in 
rate of Extended Sickness Benefits from 1st April, 1976 and (ii) 
increased amounts that becomes payable on account of coverage 
of higher wage groups from 30-11-75 and regrouping of wage 
groups arising out of amendment to ESI Act has also been taken 
into account. Due allowances has also been made for commence- 
ment of benefit periods in the new areas expected to be covered 
under the Scheme. Tho capitalised value of total liabilities of 
the permanent (partial and total), Disablement & Dependauts’ 
benefits already arisen/expected to arise out of the employment 
injuries occurring in the course of the year have also been pro- 
vided for. 

23. ADMINISTRATION EXPENSES 

The Administration expenses have been exhibited under two 
heads, viz. "(A)— Superintendence” and “(B)— Field Work". 

24(a) Provision on account of pay and allowances has been 
made for posts which have already been sanctioned as also for 
posts required on further extension and for certain new centres 

etc. 

(b) A total provision of Rs. 981.43 lacs has been made in the 
Budget for the year 1976-77 for Administration Expenses which 
works out pro rata to about Rs, 18.58 per insured employeo per 
annum, against Rs. 18.77 per insured employee per annum in 
the Revised Estimates of the current year. 

(c) The percentage of estimated Administration Expenses lo 
total Revenue in the Budget Estimates J 976-77 works out to 
9,22% as against 10.79% in tho Revised Estimates 1975-76.

The Employees’ Stale Insurance Corporation is an autonomous Organisation, It was set up under the Employees’ State Insurance 
rporation Act, 1948. It applies to all non-seasonal factories using power wherein 20 or more persons arc employed for wages. The 
Itue is being further extended under section 1(5) of the Act to new classes of establishments namely, power using factories employing 
und workers, and non-power factories employing 20 or more workers, shops, theatres, cinemas, hotels, restaurants, motor transport 
in tidings and News-paper Establishments employing 20 or more persons. It aims at helping the insured workers and their families 
if distress.

The Corporation provides the following benefits to the Insured Persons and their families

Medical Benefits — in kind through the ESI Hospitals and dispensaries.

(ii) Maternity benefits to female Insured Workers ;

(ill) Temporary/Permanent Disablement benefits to Insured Persons who meet with accidents during the course of employment ;

The administration of Medical Care under the Scheme is entrusted to the State Governments but the expenditure incurred by them 
on this account is shared between the Corporation and the State Governments in the ratio of 7 : 1 . In the Union Territory of Delhi, 
Medical Care is directly administered by the Corporation.

The expenditure on this activity is initially incurred by the State Governments who are in administrative control of the Medical Scheme 
except in the Union Territory of Delhi. The Corporation pays its share on quarterly basis on receipt of expenditure Statements from the 
Stato Government!. In order to ensure effective control, the corporation has fixed ceilings on medical expenditure under tho different 
categories of Medical Care and any expenditure Incurred by the Stato Governments over and above these ceilings is borne exclusively by 
them and such excess expenditure is not reflected in the Corporation budget or Accounts. The current ceilings are as follows:—

Cash Bancfits provided under the Employees' State Insurance Scheme are of two types, namely statutory j,o. those provided in the 
Employees' State Insurance Act and non-statutory which are extended by Resolutions of the Corporation, The eligibility for different 
categories of Cash Benefits is dependant upon the number of contributions paid by the employees and the rate of their wages. Roughly, 
the cash benefit on account of sickness comes to 50% of the wages, in case Disablement and Dependants' Benefits it works out to 62. 5% ’ 
oT the wages and full wages are paid in the case of Maternity Benefit to female insured workers. Funeral Benefit is paid at the rate of Rs. 
I 00/- in the event of death of an insured person. 

TO fijTOTHT TO yjTTFT flnHfrfl TOffTOT TO TO% I full I IVlf aft fifTO TOpftTT/tJWPT TFTfspff t^TOTT fron STFTT sfrfip tf fawt 

to xft^tfrPnt ^rrjf if TOTfror ^ jtTtot TOhfPror ^T | i fttmuib'l % tott to y?ro-h?it fsfnw TOroff to frofr 3^ ^ ftt

These benefits are paid to the Insured Persons or their boneficiaries directly by the Corporation through its Local/Pay offices which 
are located in almost all the industrial centres where the Scheme has been implemented. The incidence of expenditure on cash benefits 
depends on a variety of factors, e.g. state of health, industrial peace and the awareness of the workers about their entitlement to the 
benefits etc. it is, therefore, not possible to fix any physical targets.

This activity embraces the provision of artificial limbs to disabled Insured parsons with a viow to rehabilitate them in life, the pay- 
ment of fees to the me tibers of Medical Boards and appellate Medical Tribunals, payment of conveyance chargos and compensation for 
loss of wages to Insured workers when they are required to appear before Medical Referee, Medical Boards or appellate Medical Tribunals. 
Other miscellaneous, expenses incurred by the Corporation for the direct benoilt of insured workers also fall under this activity.

Under the ESI Act 1948, the administration of the medical scheme is the statutory responsibility of the State Governments. 
As such it is for them to provide necessary buildings to house the ESI Hospitals/Dispensaries. In the initial stages the Corpor- 
tion had sizeable balance of income over expenditure, whereas the State Governments were were not finding adequate resources 
to construct necessary buildings as a result of it, the Scheme was not making much head-way. The Corporation, therefore 
decided to invest available surplus of its income over expenditure in the construction of buildings for housing its own offices 
and ESI Hospitals/Dcispensaries.

The Corporation in its meetings held on 2nd Feb. and 3rd December, 1974 reviewed the Capital construction programme 
of the ESI projects in various States and approved that : —

(i) 10% of tire total revenue derived from ‘Contributions' be credited the Capital construction Reserve Fund; and 
expenditure on construction of Hospitals/Dlspensaries/Othor medical institutions and office buildings/staff quarters be 
incurred in the ratio of 8.2.

(iv) Plans and estimates for hospitals/annexes may be sanctioned in accourdance with the decision of the Corporation

The income of the Corporation is derived mainly from ‘Contributions' from the employers and employees covered 
under the Scheme, the inflow of which is more or less steady throughout the year. A major protion of its expenditure is 
on Medical Benefits and construction/maintenance of capital projects and is incurred through the State Governments. Tho 
payments towards the Corporation's share of expenditure on Medical Benefits are made on quarterly basis against the quarterly 
expenditure statements received from the State Governments and he funds for capital construction/mainlenance of Capital pro- 
jects arc provided according to demand from the executive agencies etc. Thus at times, the inflow of funds exceeds their out- 
flow.

In order to ensure that tho funds surplu-, to immediate requirements do noi remain idle, the ways and moans position 

is reviewed ovary week and the surplus funds are invested in Fixed Term Deposits of the State Bank of India, the duration 

of which depends on tho future requirements of funds. Sometimes, such investments are made for very short periods of 16 
days or more for which the State Bank of India allows interest at different rates depending on the period of deposit, Most 
of investments so made are realised during the currency of the year and utilised for defraying the expenditure, as and when 
the need arises.

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 25, 1976/ASVINA 3, 1898 [Part II— Sec, 3(ii)J 


It is not a regular activity of the Corporation to grant loans and its funds arc utilised solely for the furtherance of the 
objectives of tho Scheme. The surplus of its income over expenditure accumulated during previous Years had been utilised 
towards the construction of buildings for housing ESI Hospitals/ Annexes/Dispensaries and its offices/staff quarters in different 
States/Regions. The State Government of Maharashtra at earlier stages however, insisted that they would construct the build- 
ings to hospitals and dispensaries in tho State as their own property. The Corporation agreed to advance them necessary funds 
in tho shape of loans for this purpose. The last loan of Rs. 2.34 crores was sanctioned to them in the year 1971-72 pay- 
able in four yearly instalments. The last instalment has since been paid in 174-75. In the meantime at the request of tho 
Government of Maharashtra the policy of construction of ESI institutions in Maharashtra State was again reviewed and it 
was decided that the Corporation would construct future ESI Projects in this State also at its own cost. As a result of this 

construction of quite a good no. of Hospitals in Maharashtra have been sanctioned for construction at the solo cost of the 

Corporation. The Government of Maharashtra, however, approached the Corporation for expansion of existing ESI Projects 
constructed by it out of the loan already paid at the sole cost of the Corporation but as the joint ownership involved comp- 
lications it was decidod to agree to further loan to the State Government for expansion in such projects. As such a loan of 

Rs. 23,58,500/- for such projects has been provided 1975-76 and Rs. 4,56,500/- in 1976-77.
